Let's formulate objective on p normalized sum=1. We can parameterize logs: y_i = log(p_i) maybe with bounds. Use scipy minimize over y with objective = smooth_power_mean(c). But ensuring sum=1 via softmax: p=softmax(y). No constraints, but can have narrow distribution if y extreme. Add regularization? Not needed.

Smooth objective O(y)= (1/α) log ∑ exp(α c_k) where c = conv(p,p). As α→∞ approximates max c. Score=2n O. For α too large, exp overflow; use shifted: m=max c; O = (1/α)(m + log ∑ exp(α(c-m))). Gradient weights w_k = exp(α(c-m))/Z. c in [0,1] (sum=1, max≤1). α can be 100-1000. For stable: if α > 1000 maybe weights spike. Start low, anneal.

Gradient wrt y:
We need v_i = ∂/∂p_i of W(c) where W=(1/α)log ∑ e^{α c}. ∂W/∂c_k = w_k.
Let g=w. Derivative wrt p: u_i = ∑_k g_k (p_{k-i}+p_{i-k}) (within bounds).
Then ∂W/∂y_i = ∑_j ∂W/∂p_j * ∂p_j/∂y_i = p_i ∑_j u_j (δ_{ij}-p_j) = p_i (u_i - ∑_j u_j p_j).
Can compute dot = u·p.

def objective_and_grad(y, n, alpha):
 p=softmax(y)
 c=linear_conv_fft(p,p)
 m=c.max()
 shifted=alpha*(c-m)
 Z=np.exp(shifted).sum()  # since shifted≤0 safe
 weights=np.exp(shifted)/Z
 score_est=2*n*(m + np.log(Z)/alpha)  # smooth max
 u=conv_deriv(p,weights,n)
 dot = np.dot(u,p)
 grad_y = p*(u - dot)
 # add small regularization? maybe -lambda * w log p to avoid extreme? Not necessary.
 return score_est, grad_y

```
def local_search(p, n, time_limit, rng):
  best=p; bs=fast_score(p)
  while time < limit:
    cand = best.copy()
    # random log normal perturb blocks
    width = ...
    cand[start:stop] *= exp(rng.normal(0,sigma))
    cand = np.clip(cand, 1e-12, None)
    cand /= cand.sum()
    sc = fast_score(cand)
    if sc < bs - 1e-12: update
    # occasionally multi-block
    print
```

Let's think of KKT. At optimum T, there are weights λ_k on active lags such that for all i with p_i>0, ∑_k λ_k (p_{k-i}+p_{i-k})/T? constant. This is like p is eigenvector of a Toeplitz matrix built from active λ. The SOTA LP: for current f and M, maximize sum g s.t. f*g≤M. The dual λ. Then update p toward g. LP primal:
Max ∑ g_j

g_j ≥0.

Let's design functions in detail:

1. `def _next_pow2(m): return 1 << (int(np.ceil(np.log2(max(1,m)))))`
But no log? use `(1 << (m-1).bit_length())`.

2. `def _fft_convolve(a,b):`
```
n=len(a); m=len(b); size=n+m-1; nfft=_next_pow2(size)
A=scipy.fft.fft(a,nfft); B=scipy.fft.fft(b,nfft)
c=scipy.fft.ifft(A*B).real[:size]
if a is b: maybe A*A.
return c
```
Use `np.fft` if scipy not? Allowed scipy. Use `scipy.fft` for speed. Could fallback? Not needed.

3. `def _softmax(y): y = y - y.max(); e=np.exp(y); s=e.sum(); return e/s`

4. `def _conv_deriv(p, w):`
```
n=len(p)
nfft=_next_pow2(3*n-2)
P=scipy.fft.fft(p,nfft)
G=scipy.fft.fft(w,nfft)
conv2 = scipy.fft.ifft(P*G).real[:3*n-2]
Grev=scipy.fft.fft(w[::-1],nfft)
conv1 = scipy.fft.ifft(P*Grev).real[:3*n-2]
term2=conv2[:n]
term1=conv1[2*n-2 : n-1 : -1]
return term1+term2
```
Need ensure w length 2n-1. If small n, nfft ok. Could precompute nfft for n to avoid repeated. Maybe use a dict or pass in `fft_cache`. Since helper top-level, can use global dictionary? Allowed. To avoid closures, use global `FFT_CACHE = {}`. But global mutable okay. Simpler compute each time; overhead negligible.

But `w[::-1]` creates copy length. OK.

5. `def objective_grad(y, n, alpha):`
```
p=_softmax(y)
c=_fft_convolve(p,p)
c=np.clip(c,0,1.0)
m=float(c.max())
shift=alpha*(c-m)
Z=np.exp(shift).sum()
weights=np.exp(shift)/Z
val=m+np.log(Z)/alpha
u=_conv_deriv(p,weights)
dot=float(np.dot(u,p))
grad=p*(u-dot)
return val, grad
```
Could if alpha==0? Not. Use float64.

Potential numerical issue: If y range >700, softmax ok after max shift. But y may drift; we can center y by subtracting mean? Since softmax invariant, we can periodically `y -= y.mean()` to avoid large. In optimizer, variables can drift; L-BFGS-B okay. We can add a small regularization to keep sum y? Not.

6. `def score_from_p(p):`
```
p=np.asarray(p); p=np.clip(p,0,None); s=p.sum(); if s<=0: return inf; p=p/s; c=_fft_convolve(p,p); return float(2*len(p)*c.max())
```
Clip negative from FFT? max real tiny negative not issue; c may have negative small, max positive.

7. `def local_search(p, n, rng, time_limit, p_precise=False):` Use exact or FFT? Use FFT for speed, exact occasionally.
```
best=p.copy(); best_s=score_from_p(best)
sigma=0.1
no_improve=0
while time < time_limit:
  cand=best.copy()
  # choose 1 to 3 blocks
  for _ in range(rng.integers(1,3)):
     width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//64), max(2,n//8)))
     start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
     cand[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma))
  cand=np.clip(cand,1e-15,1000)
  cand/=cand.sum()
  s=score_from_p(cand)
  if s < best_s - 1e-12:
     best=cand; best_s=s; no_improve=0; update sigma maybe
  else: no_improve +=1
  # if long no improve, random restart? maybe perturb best more
```
Could be slow if many FFT for n=512. But okay.

We need exact evaluation of best candidates. The final exact might differ due FFT rounding. But likely same order.

```
def adam_step(p, grad, m_v, v_v, t, lr):
  m_v=0.9*m_v+0.1*grad
  v_v=0.999*v_v+0.001*grad*grad
  bias_m=1-0.9**t; bias_v=1-0.999**t
  step=lr*m_v/bias_m/np.sqrt(v_v/bias_v+1e-8)
  return p - step, m_v, v_v
```

```
def exact_local_search(p, deadline, seed):
```

```
def make_starts(n, rng, budget):
  starts=[uniform]
  # parent if available: downsample/upsample
  # sinusoids: for phase, harmk, A
  x=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
  for A in [0.5,0.8,0.99]:
    for harm in [1,2]:
      for phase in [0, np.pi/2, np.pi/4, 3*np.pi/4]:
        f=1 + A*np.sin(2*np.pi*harm*x + phase)
        f=np.clip(f,1e-8, None); starts.append(f/f.sum())
  # ramp/curvature
  t=(np.arange(n)-0.5)/(n)
  for slope in [-1, -0.5, 0.5,1]: f=1+slope*t; f=clip; starts.append
  for curv in [-1,1]: f=1+curv*((t)**2-1/3); clip; starts.append
  # endpoint blocks: p0 and p_{n-1} high? f=1 + A*(cos pi t?) nonnegative? f=1 + A*cos(2π t) maybe
  # random starts 2
  starts.extend(random softmax)
  # deduplicate? not necessary
```

Now, LP function:
```
def lp_update(p, n, M, time_limit=5):
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    from scipy.sparse import lil_matrix
    A = lil_matrix((2*n-1, n))
    for j in range(n):
       val=p[j]
       # lags k=j .. j+n-1? coefficient p[k-j]=p[len? Wait coefficient for variable g_j in constraint k is p_{k-j} if 0≤k-j<n. For fixed j, k ranges j..j+n-1, coefficient p_{k-j}=p_0..p_{n-1}. So column j is p shifted.
       A[j:j+n, j] = p  # length n
    A=A.tocsr()
    b=np.full(2*n-1, M*(1+1e-9))
    c=np.ones(n); c=-1
    res=linprog(c,A,A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
    if res.success:
       g=res.x
       if g.sum()>0: g/=g.sum(); return g
    return None
```
Wait constraint k: ∑_j p_{k-j} g_j. For variable g_j, p_{k-j} index 0..n-1. When k-j = i, k=j+i. Yes coefficient column j at row j+i is p_i. So A[j:j+n,j]=p. Good.

Now, exact local search implementation. To avoid excessive exact calls for large n, set `max_exact_evals` or time.
```
def exact_polish(p, deadline, seed):
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   best=p.copy(); best_s=evaluate_sequence(best.tolist())
   sigma=0.1
   n=len(best)
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       cand=best.copy()
       for _ in range(rng.integers(1,3)):
           width=int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//32)))
           start=int(rng.integers(0, n-width))
           cand[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma))
       cand=np.clip(cand,0,None)
       s=cand.sum()
       if s<=0: continue
       cand /= s
       sc=evaluate_sequence(cand.tolist())
       if sc<best_s:
           best=cand; best_s=sc; print
       else:
           sigma = max(0.05, sigma*0.99)
           if random<0.01: sigma*1.2 etc
   return best,best_s
```
But exact evaluator copies list and convolve. For n=2048, tolist 2048 and conv 4M. Maybe 50-100 evals. We can limit time.

Could implement simple gradient descent with Adam for high n to avoid L-BFGS overhead. Let's add a custom Adam optimizer after L-BFGS:
```
def adam_optimize(y, n, alpha, time_limit, rng):
   m=np.zeros_like(y); v=np.zeros_like(y); t=0
   lr=0.05
   while time<limit:
      val, grad=objective_grad(y,n,alpha)
      if not finite: break
      t+=1; m=0.9*m+0.1*grad; v=0.999*v+0.001*grad*grad
      bm=1-0.9**t; bv=1-0.999**t
      step=lr*m/bm/np.sqrt(v/bv+1e-8)
      y_new=y-step
      # optional step scaling for stability: if objective worsens too much, reduce
      y=y_new - y.mean()? center
   p=softmax(y)
```
But learning rate tricky. Grad scale? Objective c ~1/n, grad wrt y maybe p*... ~1/n? For n=512, c ~0.002, grad maybe 0.002? lr 1 maybe. We can use line search? Maybe skip; L-BFGS robust.

But L-BFGS may be slow for full n; Adam could complement. We can implement simple backtracking gradient:
```
def grad_desc(y,n,alpha,time_limit, step_min, step_max):
  step=0.5
  val,grad=objective_grad(...)
  while time<limit:
    y_new=y - step*grad
    val_new,_=objective_grad(y_new)
    if finite and val_new < val:
       y=y_new; val=val_new; step*=1.05
    else:
       step*=0.5
```

Let's design LP-specific algorithm:
- Start with p (normalized). Compute M=max(p*p).
- Solve LP max sum g s.t. p*g≤M, g≥0.
- If g_sum <=1+tol, maybe no improvement; try M*(1+eps) or perturb.
- Scale g to sum1. Update p = (1-t)p + t*g for t in [0.2,0.5,0.8]. Choose update with lowest M. Iterate until time/no improvement.
Because p positive, LP feasible bounded? Is it bounded with p positive? Yes: Each g_j constrained by constraints involving p_{?}. For variable g_j, constraints k=j+i: ∑? includes p_i g_j plus other. The smallest coefficient over i? If p_i positive, choosing k where coefficient min? The constraint with i=0? Depending. Since p_i min >0 (softmax), each g_j ≤ M / min_i? There is bound. Good.

This LP only uses one-step linearization. It might converge to a local optimum where for active peaks, KKT. Could be more targeted than smooth.

Implement:
```
def lp_direction(p, M):
    n=len(p)
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    cols=np.arange(n)
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j+n, j] = p
    b=np.full(2*n-1, M)
    c=np.full(n,-1.0)
    res=linprog(c, A, None, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
    if res.success: return res.x
    # if unbounded? maybe set bound 10
```
But variable bounds list of tuples for n large maybe. Use `bounds=(0, 10)`? If we cap g≤10, objective bounded. Feasible p sum1, g≤10 enough? If true optimum large, cap prevents. But we only need some improving direction; cap may be okay. Use `bounds=(0, 10.0)`.

If p has min very small (softmax), constraints weak, g could large but cap. Use floor p? Since p=softmax min maybe 1e-30 if y extreme; LP constraints weak, unbounded/cap. We can ensure p flattening by adding floor: `p_lp = p + eps; p_lp/=p_lp.sum()` with eps=1e-8 maybe. This keeps min. Objective M recompute? Use original M slightly increased.

We can run LP loop:
```
def lp_optimize(p, n, time_limit, rng):
   best=p.copy(); best_m=score_fft(p)/(2n)
   for it in range(200):
      M = score_fft(best)/(2n)*(1+1e-8)
      g = lp_direction(best, M)
      if not g or g.sum()<1+1e-6: break
      gs = g/g.sum()
      improved=False
      for t in [0.1,0.3,0.6,1.0]:
         cand=(1-t)*best+t*gs; cand/=cand.sum()
         m=score_fft(cand)/(2n)
         if m < best_m-1e-12:
            best=cand; best_m=m; improved=True; break
      if not improved:
         # try randomized sym/clip? break or T=1?
         break
   return best
```
But if no improvement for t, maybe g direction not lowering; could use t with some random. Break.

Cost LP per iteration high. Limit iterations 10. Use for n=256 maybe. Starts random.

`_smooth_objective_and_grad(x,n,alpha)`:
```
p=_softmax(x)
c=_fft_square(p)
c=np.clip(c,0,1.0)
m=float(c.max())
if m<=0: return 0, zeros

Z=np.exp(shifted).sum()
weights=np.exp(shifted)/Z
val=m+np.log(Z)/alpha
u=_conv_deriv(p,weights)
dot=float(np.dot(u,p))
grad=p*(u-dot)
# sanitize

```

`_conv_deriv(p,w)` with FFT:
```
n=len(p); nfft=_get_nfft_d(n)
P=scipy.fft.fft(p,nfft)
G=scipy.fft.fft(w,nfft)
conv2 = scipy.fft.ifft(P*G).real
term2 = conv2[:n]
Grev=scipy.fft.fft(w[::-1],nfft)
conv1=scipy.fft.ifft(P*Grev).real
term1=conv1[2*n-2 : n-1 : -1]
u=term1+term2
u[~finite]=0
return u
```

`_local_search_fft`: use random block multiplicative. Need avoid huge time. Use `while time.monotonic() < stop:`. We'll pass `stop_time`.
```
def _local_search_fft(p, stop_time, rng):
    n=len(p); best=p.copy(); best_s=_score_fft(best)
    sigma=0.15
    i=0
    while time.monotonic() < stop_time:
       i+=1
       sizes = [int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//16+1))) for _ in range(rng.integers(1,3))]
       cand=best.copy()
       for w in sizes:
          if w>=n: start=0; width=n
          else: start=int(rng.integers(0, n-w+1)); width=w
          cand[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
          if rng.random()<0.1: cand[start:start+width] += sigma? no.
       cand=np.clip(cand,1e-300,None); s=cand.sum(); if s>0: cand/=s
       sc=_score_fft(cand)
       if sc < best_s - 1e-12: best=cand; best_s=sc; print? maybe not every
       elif i%200==0: sigma=max(0.05, sigma*0.98)
    return best, best_s
```
No too many prints.

```
def _add_norm(starts, f, min_eps=1e-12):
```

```
    if time>deadline: break

    if res.fun < current_val? update
```

Global cache:
```
_NFFT_CACHE = {}
def _nfft_conv(n):
    key=(1,n)
    val=_NFFT_CACHE.get(key)
    if val is None:
       val = 1 << (max(1,2*n-1).bit_length())
       _NFFT_CACHE[key]=val
    return val
def _nfft_deriv(n):
    key=(2,n)
    val=_NFFT_CACHE.get(key)
    if val is None:
       val = 1 << (max(1,3*n-2).bit_length())
       _NFFT_CACHE[key]=val
    return val
```
For m=1, bit_length of 1 is1 =>2? If size=2n-1 maybe n=1 size1; next pow2 1? `(1).bit_length()`=1, 1<<0? We can do `1 << (m-1).bit_length()`; for m=1 gives1; m=3 (2).bit_length2 ->4. Use:
```
def _next_power_of_two(m):
    m=int(max(1,m))
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```
Good.

```
c=_fft_square(p)
m=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
if m <0: m=0
return float(2*len(p)*m)
```

```
def _alphas_for(n):
    vals=[float(n)//4+20, float(n)+50, 5*float(n)+200, 20*float(n)+1000]
    out=[]
    for v in vals:
       v= max(20.0, min(v, 100000.0))
       out.append(v)
    return out
```

```
def _optimize_length(n, stop_time, rng, extra):

  top = 1 if n>1024 else 3

     if time >= stop_time: break
     if i>0 and time.monotonic() > stop_time - 20: break
     y=np.log(...)
     cur_p=start; cur_s=bs if i==0? Actually start score.
     for alpha in alphas[:2 if n>1024 else 4]:
        for run in range(1 if n>1024 else 2):
           if time... break
           res=...
           if finite:
              p_new=_softmax(res.x)
              s_new=_score_fft(p_new)
              if s_new < cur_s: y=res.x; cur_s=s_new; cur_p=p_new
              # else keep y? maybe if res.x still could help with higher alpha, set y=res.x only if finite? Could move away. Use if s_new <= cur_s*1.05 (not much worse) to explore. But to avoid bad, set y=res.x always if finite? Could be worse but maybe for next alpha. Let's set if finite: y=res.x; if s_new<cur_s update.
     p=_softmax(y); s=_score_fft(p)
     if s<bs: best=p; bs=s
  # local search final if time left
```

Template list code:
```
def _make_starts(n, rng, extra_seq=None):
    raw=[]
    raw.append(np.ones(n))
    if extra_seq is not None:
       raw.append(_resample(extra_seq,n))
    parent=_get_parent()
    if parent is not None:
       raw.append(_resample(parent,n))
    t=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    tp=t-0.5
    slopes=[-1.0,-0.5,0.5,1.0]
    for a in slopes: raw.append(1 + a*t)
    for a in slopes: raw.append(1 + a*tp*tp)
    for harm in (1,2,3):
       for A in (0.5,0.9):
          for ph in (0.0, 0.25*np.pi, 0.5*np.pi, 0.75*np.pi):
```

`_resample(a,n)` normalize. If a size zero return ones.
```
def _resample(a,n):
    arr=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    arr=np.clip(arr,0,None)
    if arr.size==0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)): return np.ones(n)
    if arr.size==n: out=arr
    else:
       x_old=np.linspace(0,1,arr.size)
       x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
       out=np.interp(x_new,x_old,arr)
    out=np.clip(out,0,None); s=out.sum(); if s<=0: return np.ones(n); return out/s
```

`_lp_direction` dense matrix. Use `from scipy.optimize import linprog` inside top? We can import at top. If linprog fails, return None.
```
def _lp_direction(p, M):
    n=len(p)
    if n > 800: return None
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j+n, j] = p
    b=np.full(2*n-1, M*(1.0+1e-7))
    c=-np.ones(n)
    bounds=(0.0, 10.0)
    try:
       res=scipy.optimize.linprog(c, A, None, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res is not None and res.success:
       g=np.asarray(res.x,float)
       if np.all(np.isfinite(g)) and g.sum()>0:
          return g
    return None
```
`bounds` tuple applies to all variables? linprog accepts sequence or bounds as `bounds=(0,10)`? It accepts sequence of (min,max) or dict; tuple of two numbers maybe means for all? In scipy linprog, `bounds` can be sequence of (min,max) or dict; a tuple like (0,10) may be interpreted as bounds for first variable? Actually docs: `bounds : sequence of Sequence, optional`. To specify same for all, use `[(0,10)]*n`. But list multiplication with tuples ok. For n=512, create list. Use `bounds = [(0.0, 10.0)] * n`. No lambda.

LP polish:
```
def _lp_polish(p, stop_time, rng):
    n=len(p); best=p.copy(); best_s=_score_fft(best)
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < stop_time:
       M= _score_fft(best)/(2*n) * (1+1e-8)
       g=_lp_direction(best, M)
       if g is None or g.sum() <= 1+1e-6:
          break
       g=g/g.sum()
       improved=False
       for t in (0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0):
          cand=(1-t)*best + t*g
          cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); s=cand.sum(); if s>0: cand/=s
          sc=_score_fft(cand)
          if sc < best_s - 1e-10:
             best=cand; best_s=sc; improved=True; break
       if not improved:
          break
       it+=1
    return best
```
No RNG.

Maybe if g sum is huge due cap, scaling. Good.

```
def _exact_polish(p, stop_time, rng):
    best=_normalize...(p)
    best_s=_exact_score(best)
    max_evals = 300 if len(best)<600 else 80
    count=0
    while time.monotonic()<stop_time and count<max_evals:
       cand=best.copy()
       width=int(rng.integers(1, max(2, len(best)//32+1)))
       start=int(rng.integers(0, len(best)-width+1))
       cand[start:start+width]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.2))
       cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); s=cand.sum(); if s>0: cand/=s
       sc=_exact_score(cand); count+=1
       if sc<best_s:
          best=cand; best_s=sc; print
    return best, best_s
```

In `_optimize_length`, before log, clean p: `p=np.clip(np.asarray(start,float),1e-12,None); s=p.sum(); if not finite: continue`. Do that.

Now, exact target: If best_exact is inf due evaluate not defined in dev, final fallback? The judge defines.

Let's include an initial exact evaluation of uniform if parent none, to set best_seq. In propose:
```
if best_seq is None:
    best_seq=np.ones(128); best_exact=_exact_score(best_seq)
```
before lengths maybe. This ensures valid.

Now, time: If parent exact for length4806 at start takes 0.2s. Good.

Potential problem with `np.interp` when arr.size=1 and n>1: x_old [0], returns constant. Good.

Potential problem with sequence length in returned: If best_seq length huge (4806) and exact polish does evals, okay. If best_seq length 2048, good.

Let's craft final code. Ensure docstring at top. Use prints.

One more idea: Since if lower bound 2, returning all ones length 1 maybe simple exact 2. But our returned sum1 length maybe 4806 all ones also 2. If optimizer fails, best_seq parent 4806 normalized maybe score2. Good.

Now final code. We'll not include analysis. Ensure final only fenced code.
</think>

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import scipy.optimize


"""
Searches for low-score non-negative step-function coefficients for the evaluator

    score = 2*n*max(a*a) / (sum(a)**2)

where a*a is the exact linear convolution and a is forced non-negative.

Strategy:
- Normalize sequences to sum 1, so the score is 2*n*max(convolution).
- Use FFT-based surrogates for fast objective/gradient evaluations.
- Optimize a smooth log-sum-exp power mean of the convolution peaks with
  L-BFGS over a softmax parametrization.
- Add template starts (uniform, parent resampling, ramps, sinusoids, endpoint
  shapes, random starts), local block-perturbation polishing, and an optional
  LP-based active-set direction inspired by the previous SOTA.
- Periodically verify candidates with the authoritative evaluate_sequence()
  and end with an exact evaluation/local polish.
"""

_NFFT_CACHE = {}


def _next_power_of_two(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _get_nfft_conv(n):
    key = (1, int(n))
    val = _NFFT_CACHE.get(key)
    if val is None:
        val = _next_power_of_two(2 * int(n) - 1)
        _NFFT_CACHE[key] = val
    return val


def _get_nfft_deriv(n):
    key = (2, int(n))
    val = _NFFT_CACHE.get(key)
    if val is None:
        val = _next_power_of_two(3 * int(n) - 2)
        _NFFT_CACHE[key] = val
    return val


def _fft_square(p):
    n = len(p)
    nfft = _get_nfft_conv(n)
    P = np.fft.fft(p, nfft)
    out = np.fft.ifft(P * P).real[:2 * n - 1]
    return np.clip(out, 0.0, None)


def _fft_convolve(a, b):
    n = len(a)
    m = len(b)
    nfft = _next_power_of_two(n + m - 1)
    A = np.fft.fft(a, nfft)
    B = np.fft.fft(b, nfft)
    return np.fft.ifft(A * B).real[:n + m - 1]


def _normalize_nonnegative(a):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, None)
    if arr.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        return None
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return arr / s


def _score_fft(p):
    arr = _normalize_nonnegative(p)
    if arr is None:
        return float("inf")
    c = _fft_square(arr)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2 * len(arr) * m)


def _softmax(y):
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
        n = y.size
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n)
    y = y - float(np.max(y))
    e = np.exp(y)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(y.size, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, y.size)
    return e / s


def _convolution_derivative(p, w):
    """
    Derivative of sum_k w_k (p*p)_k with respect to p.

    For c = p*p, dc_k/dp_i = p_{k-i} + p_{i-k}, where invalid indices are zero.
    """
    n = len(p)
    nfft = _get_nfft_deriv(n)
    P = np.fft.fft(p, nfft)
    G = np.fft.fft(w, nfft)

    conv_forward = np.fft.ifft(P * G).real
    term_forward = conv_forward[:n]

    G_rev = np.fft.fft(w[::-1], nfft)
    conv_reverse = np.fft.ifft(P * G_rev).real
    # Need indices 2*n-2, 2*n-3, ..., n-1.
    term_reverse = conv_reverse[2 * n - 2 : n - 1 : -1]

    out = term_forward + term_reverse
    out[~np.isfinite(out)] = 0.0
    return out


def _smooth_objective_and_grad(y, n, alpha):
    """
    Smooth approximation to max_k (p*p)_k, where p = softmax(y).
    Returns (value, gradient wrt y).
    """
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if y.size != int(n) or not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
        return 1e100, np.zeros(int(n), dtype=np.float64)

    p = _softmax(y)
    c = _fft_square(p)
    if c.size == 0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(int(n), dtype=np.float64)

    c = np.clip(c, 0.0, 1.0)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return 1e100, np.zeros(int(n), dtype=np.float64)

    alpha = float(max(1.0, alpha))
    shifted = alpha * (c - m)
    exp_shift = np.exp(shifted)
    Z = float(exp_shift.sum())
    if Z <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(Z):
        weights = np.zeros_like(c)
        weights[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        Zval_log = 0.0
    else:
        weights = exp_shift / Z
        Zval_log = float(np.log(Z))

    val = m + Zval_log / alpha
    u = _convolution_derivative(p, weights)
    dot = float(np.dot(u, p))
    grad = p * (u - dot)
    grad[~np.isfinite(grad)] = 0.0

    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return 1e100, np.zeros(int(n), dtype=np.float64)
    return float(val), grad


def _fun_smooth(x, n, alpha):
    val, grad = _smooth_objective_and_grad(x, n, alpha)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return 1e100, np.zeros_like(x, dtype=np.float64)
    return val, grad


def _alphas_for(n):
    n = float(max(16, int(n)))
    vals = [
        0.25 * n + 10.0,
        n + 10.0,
        5.0 * n + 50.0,
        20.0 * n + 200.0,
    ]
    out = []
    for v in vals:
        v = max(20.0, min(v, 100000.0))
        out.append(v)
    return out


def _get_parent():
    return globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)


def _resample(a, n):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, None)
    if arr.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)

    if arr.size == n:
        out = arr.copy()
    else:
        x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, arr.size, dtype=np.float64)
        x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
        out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, arr).astype(np.float64)

    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, None)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    return out / s


def _sort_first_item(item):
    return item[0]


def _make_starts(n, rng, extra_seq=None):
    n = int(max(16, n))
    raw = []

    raw.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n))

    if extra_seq is not None:
        raw.append(_resample(extra_seq, n))

    par = _get_parent()
    if par is not None:
        raw.append(_resample(par, n))

    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    tp = t - 0.5

    for a in (-1.0, -0.5, 0.5, 1.0):
        raw.append(1.0 + a * t)
        raw.append(1.0 + a * (t - 1.0) * (t - 1.0))
        raw.append(1.0 + a * tp * tp)

    for harm in (1, 2, 3):
        for amp in (0.45, 0.85):
            for ph in (0.0, 0.25 * np.pi, 0.5 * np.pi, 0.75 * np.pi):
                raw.append(1.0 + amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * harm * t + ph))
                raw.append(1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * harm * t + ph))

    # Endpoint-enhanced / center-enhanced smooth shapes.
    denom_base = 4.0 * t * (1.0 - t)
    for a in (0.75, 1.5, 3.0, -0.5):
        raw.append(1.0 / (1.0 + a * denom_base))

    # Random positive starts.
    for _ in range(3):
        raw.append(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n).astype(np.float64)))

    all_p = []
    for f in raw:
        arr = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size != n:
            arr = _resample(arr, n)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 1e-12, None)
        s = float(arr.sum())
        if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
            all_p.append(arr / s)

    if not all_p:
        return [np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)]

    scored = []
    for idx, p in enumerate(all_p):
        scored.append((_score_fft(p), idx))
    scored.sort(key=_sort_first_item)

    limit = min(10, len(scored))
    return [all_p[idx] for _, idx in scored[:limit]]


def _local_search_fft(p, stop_time, rng):
    p = _normalize_nonnegative(p)
    if p is None:
        return None
    best = p.copy()
    best_s = _score_fft(best)
    n = len(best)
    if n <= 0 or not np.isfinite(best_s):
        return best

    sigma = 0.14
    it = 0
    while time.monotonic() < stop_time:
        it += 1
        cand = best.copy()
        nblocks = int(rng.integers(1, 3))
        for _ in range(nblocks):
            max_width = max(2, n // 32 + 1)
            w = int(rng.integers(1, max_width + 1))
            if w >= n:
                start = 0
                w = n
            else:
                start = int(rng.integers(0, n - w + 1))
            factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
            cand[start:start + w] *= factor

        cand = np.clip(cand, 1e-300, None)
        s = float(cand.sum())
        if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
            continue
        cand /= s
        sc = _score_fft(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s - 1e-13:
            best = cand
            best_s = sc
            sigma = min(0.35, sigma * 1.08)
        else:
            sigma = max(0.04, sigma * 0.985)

    return best


def _lp_direction(p, M):
    """
    Solve the SOTA LP direction:
        max sum(g)
        s.t. (p*g)_k <= M for all k, g >= 0.
    A global bound is added to avoid numerical unboundedness.
    """
    n = len(p)
    if n < 8 or n > 700:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)) or M <= 0.0:
        return None

    A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j + n, j] = p

    b = np.full(2 * n - 1, float(M) * (1.0 + 1e-8), dtype=np.float64)
    c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, 10.0)] * n

    try:
        res = scipy.optimize.linprog(
            c,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"presolve": True}
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is not None and getattr(res, "success", False):
        g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if g.size == n and np.all(np.isfinite(g)) and float(g.sum()) > 0.0:
            return g
    return None


def _lp_polish(p, stop_time, rng=None):
    p = _normalize_nonnegative(p)
    if p is None:
        return None
    best = p.copy()
    best_s = _score_fft(best)
    n = len(best)

    while time.monotonic() < stop_time:
        M = _score_fft(best) / max(1e-300, 2.0 * n)
        g = _lp_direction(best, max(M, 1e-12))
        if g is None:
            break
        gs = float(g.sum())
        if gs <= 1.0 + 1e-7:
            break
        g = g / gs

        improved = False
        for tau in (0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0):
            cand = (1.0 - tau) * best + tau * g
            cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, None)
            s = float(cand.sum())
            if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
                continue
            cand /= s
            sc = _score_fft(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s - 1e-12:
                best = cand
                best_s = sc
                improved = True
                break
        if not improved:
            break
    return best


def _optimize_length(n, stop_time, rng, extra_seq=None):
    n = int(max(16, n))
    starts = _make_starts(n, rng, extra_seq)
    if not starts:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)

    best = None
    best_s = float("inf")

    # Choose a few starts: best, another good one, and one exploratory one.
    chosen_idxs = []
    if starts:
        chosen_idxs.append(0)
        if len(starts) > 1:
            chosen_idxs.append(min(1, len(starts) - 1))
        if len(starts) > 1:
            chosen_idxs.append(int(rng.integers(0, len(starts))))

    chosen = []
    seen = set()
    for idx in chosen_idxs:
        idx = int(idx)
        if 0 <= idx < len(starts) and idx not in seen:
            seen.add(idx)
            chosen.append(starts[idx])

    if not chosen:
        chosen = [starts[0]]

    max_chosen = 1 if n > 1600 else (2 if n > 600 else 3)
    chosen = chosen[:max_chosen]

    alphas = _alphas_for(n)
    n_alpha = 2 if n > 1200 else 4
    n_win = 1 if n > 1200 else 2

    for start in chosen:
        if time.monotonic() >= stop_time:
            break

        p0 = np.asarray(start, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        p0 = np.clip(p0, 1e-15, None)
        s0 = float(p0.sum())
        if s0 <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s0):
            continue
        p0 = p0 / s0

        y = np.log(np.clip(p0, 1e-300, None))
        cur_p = p0.copy()
        cur_s = _score_fft(cur_p)

        for alpha in alphas[:n_alpha]:
            for _ in range(n_win):
                if time.monotonic() >= stop_time:
                    break

                maxfun = 60 if n > 1200 else (120 if n > 500 else 180)
                maxiter = 10 if n > 1200 else 20

                try:
                    res = scipy.optimize.minimize(
                        _fun_smooth,
                        y,
                        args=(n, alpha),
                        jac=True,
                        method="L-BFGS-B",
                        options={
                            "maxiter": maxiter,
                            "maxfun": maxfun,
                            "ftol": 1e-12,
                            "gtol": 1e-8,
                        }
                    )
                except Exception:
                    break

                if res is None:
                    break

                res_x = getattr(res, "x", None)
                if res_x is None or not np.all(np.isfinite(res_x)):
                    break

                y = res_x
                p_new = _softmax(res_x)
                sc_new = _score_fft(p_new)
                if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < cur_s:
                    cur_s = sc_new
                    cur_p = p_new

        p_final = _softmax(y)
        sc_final = _score_fft(p_final)
        if np.isfinite(sc_final) and sc_final < best_s:
            best = np.asarray(p_final, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            best_s = sc_final

    if best is None:
        best = np.asarray(chosen[0], dtype=np.float64).copy()
        best_s = _score_fft(best)

    if time.monotonic() < stop_time - 1.0:
        # Moderate local polishing, then LP-inspired polish for moderate n.
        local_stop = min(stop_time, time.monotonic() + min(8.0, max(2.0, (stop_time - time.monotonic()) * 0.2)))
        best2 = _local_search_fft(best, local_stop, rng)
        if best2 is not None:
            s2 = _score_fft(best2)
            if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_s:
                best = best2
                best_s = s2

        if n <= 700 and time.monotonic() < stop_time - 2.0:
            lp_stop = min(stop_time, time.monotonic() + min(20.0, max(2.0, (stop_time - time.monotonic()) * 0.3)))
            best3 = _lp_polish(best, lp_stop, rng)
            if best3 is not None:
                s3 = _score_fft(best3)
                if np.isfinite(s3) and s3 < best_s:
                    best = best3
                    best_s = s3

    return best


def _exact_score(p):
    arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if arr.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        return float("inf")
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return float("inf")
    arr = arr / s
    try:
        val = float(evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return float("inf")
    return val


def _exact_polish(p, stop_time, rng):
    arr = _normalize_nonnegative(p)
    if arr is None:
        return None
    best = arr.copy()
    best_s = _exact_score(best)

    n = len(best)
    if n > 2500:
        max_evals = 25
    elif n > 1000:
        max_evals = 60
    elif n > 400:
        max_evals = 120
    else:
        max_evals = 300

    count = 0
    sigma = 0.12

    while time.monotonic() < stop_time and count < max_evals:
        cand = best.copy()
        max_width = max(2, n // 48 + 1)
        w = int(rng.integers(1, max_width + 1))
        if w >= n:
            start = 0
            w = n
        else:
            start = int(rng.integers(0, n - w + 1))

        cand[start:start + w] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
        cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, None)
        s = float(cand.sum())
        if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
            continue
        cand /= s

        # Pre-filter with FFT score to avoid unnecessary heavy exact evals.
        fft_s = _score_fft(cand)
        if not (fft_s < best_s + 0.05):
            continue

        sc = _exact_score(cand)
        count += 1
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s:
            best = cand
            best_s = sc
            sigma = min(0.30, sigma * 1.08)
            print("[exact-polish] improved to", best_s)
        else:
            sigma = max(0.04, sigma * 0.985)

    return best, float(best_s) if np.isfinite(best_s) else float("inf")


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=400, **kwargs):
    """Main entry point: returns the best non-negative sequence found."""
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    try:
        total_budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        total_budget = 400.0
    if not np.isfinite(total_budget) or total_budget <= 0.0:
        total_budget = 400.0

    hard_deadline = start_time + max(1.0, total_budget)
    work_deadline = hard_deadline - 12.0
    if work_deadline <= start_time + 5.0:
        work_deadline = hard_deadline - max(0.5, min(2.0, total_budget * 0.05))

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if np.isfinite(seed) else 42)

    best_seq = None
    best_exact = float("inf")

    def consider(seq, source):
        nonlocal_best_placeholder = None  # placeholder not used
        # We deliberately avoid nested modifications by returning values.
        return None

    # Evaluate parent construction first, if available and not huge.
    par = _get_parent()
    if par is not None:
        try:
            ppar = _resample(par, max(16, int(len(np.asarray(par, dtype=np.float64).ravel()))))
            if ppar is not None and len(ppar) <= 6000:
                sc = _exact_score(ppar)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact:
                    best_seq = ppar.copy()
                    best_exact = sc
                    print("[parent] exact score:", best_exact)
        except Exception:
            pass

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0
        best_exact = _exact_score(best_seq)
        print("[initial uniform] exact score:", best_exact)

    # Build a prioritized list of lengths to try.
    lengths_set = set()
    par_len = 0
    if par is not None:
        try:
            par_len = int(len(np.asarray(par, dtype=np.float64).ravel()))
            if 32 <= par_len <= 3000:
                lengths_set.add(par_len)
        except Exception:
            par_len = 0

    for n in (128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 64):
        if time.monotonic() < work_deadline - 25.0:
            lengths_set.add(n)

    lengths = []
    for n in (128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 64):
        if n in lengths_set:
            lengths.append(n)
    for n in lengths_set:
        if n not in lengths:
            lengths.append(n)

    # Main multi-length search.
    for n in lengths:
        if time.monotonic() >= work_deadline - 5.0:
            break
        if n > 2048 and time.monotonic() >= work_deadline - 40.0:
            continue

        if n <= 256:
            suggested_stop = 50.0
        elif n <= 512:
            suggested_stop = 80.0
        elif n <= 1024:
            suggested_stop = 110.0
        else:
            suggested_stop = 140.0

        remaining = work_deadline - time.monotonic()
        stop_local = min(suggested_stop, max(8.0, remaining))
        stop_time = time.monotonic() + stop_local
        stop_time = min(stop_time, work_deadline)

        p = _optimize_length(int(n), stop_time, rng, extra_seq=best_seq)
        if p is None:
            continue

        sc = _exact_score(p)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact:
            best_seq = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            best_exact = sc
            print("[length", n, "] new exact best:", best_exact)
        else:
            print("[length", n, "] exact score not better:", sc if np.isfinite(sc) else "inf")

    # Final exact verification and polish.
    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0

    polish_deadline = hard_deadline - 1.0
    if time.monotonic() < polish_deadline:
        polished, polished_s = _exact_polish(best_seq, polish_deadline, rng)
        if polished is not None and np.isfinite(polished_s) and polished_s < best_exact:
            best_seq = polished
            best_exact = polished_s
        elif polished is not None:
            best_seq = polished
            best_exact = polished_s

    # Final safety validation: ensure a valid, finite, non-negative list.
    out = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if out.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(out)) or out.sum() <= 1e-12:
        out = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0
        s = float(out.sum())
        if s > 0:
            out = out / s

    # Keep total mass one for numerical stability; score is scale invariant.
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s > 0.0:
        out = out / s

    final_score = _exact_score(out)
    if np.isfinite(final_score) and final_score < best_exact:
        best_exact = final_score
    print("[final] returned exact score:", best_exact)

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
